the host's ``~/.claude/``\n        contents (every direct child file's mtime + SHA-256, every\n        direct child directory's mtime) MUST be byte-identical\n        post-test.\"\"\"\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=_EVAL_ID,\n            home_root=real_claude_dir,\n            session_id=\"sess-nfrsec3-direct\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            # Default config: ``~/.claude/`` is NOT in the default\n            # allowlist (``/tmp/eval-runs`` and ``.dev/eval-runs``).\n            iso.setup(config=EvalConfig())\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        # Forensic payload preserves the verbatim attempted root.\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == real_claude_dir\n\n        # NFR-SEC3 invariant: every pre-existing child of ``~/.claude/``\n        # MUST be byte-identical after the refusal. mkdtemp may have\n        # created a *new* per-eval HOME entry (cleaned up by the\n        # fixture), but it MUST NOT have modified any pre-existing\n        # entry.\n        post = _DirSnapshot.capture(real_claude_dir)\n        for name, before_entry in dot_claude_snapshot.entries.items():\n            assert name in post.entries, (\n                f\"Pre-existing entry {name!r} disappeared from ~/.claude/ \"\n                f\"after refused setup — the guard mutated the real HOME.\"\n            )\n            after_entry = post.entries[name]\n            assert after_entry == before_entry, (\n                f\"Pre-existing entry {name!r} under ~/.claude/ was modified \"\n                f\"by the refused setup. Before={before_entry!r}, \"\n                f\"after={after_entry!r}.\"\n            )\n\n        # Any newly-created entry MUST be a leaked per-eval HOME with\n        # the test module's eval-id prefix — never anything else.\n        new_entries = set(post.entries) - set(dot_claude_snapshot.entries)\n        leaked_prefix = f\"{_EVAL_ID}-\"\n        for name in new_entries:\n            assert name.startswith(leaked_prefix), (\n                f\"Unexpected new entry {name!r} appeared under ~/.claude/ \"\n                f\"after the refused setup; only entries with the \"\n                f\"{leaked_prefix!r} prefix are accepted as test leaks.\"\n            )\n\n    def test_per_eval_home_is_empty_when_setup_refuses(\n        self,\n        real_claude_dir: Path,\n        cleanup_leaked_eval_homes: None,  # noqa: ARG002 (fixture side effect)\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"The guard fires BEFORE the hook adapter (T02.14) writes\n        anything under the per-eval HOME. When ``setup()`` refuses on\n        the real ``~/.claude/`` case, the leaked per-eval HOME MUST be\n        empty (no nested files, no nested directories, no symlinks).\n\n        This is the second half of NFR-SEC3's \"refusal-before-side-\n        effects\" guarantee: the first half (real ``~/.claude/`` direct\n        children) is asserted by the snapshot test above; this half\n        asserts the per-eval HOME itself.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=_EVAL_ID,\n            home_root=real_claude_dir,\n            session_id=\"sess-nfrsec3-empty\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation):\n            iso.setup(config=EvalConfig())\n\n        # Find the leaked per-eval HOME (mkdtemp prefix + suffix).\n        leaked_prefix = f\"{_EVAL_ID}-\"\n        leaks = [\n            child\n            for child in real_claude_dir.iterdir()\n            if child.name.startswith(leaked_prefix)\n        ]\n        # There MUST be exactly one leak: a single mkdtemp ran before\n        # the guard rejected. Zero would mean the implementation\n        # silently dropped the mkdtemp (regression in the partial-HOME\n        # preservation contract); >1 would mean the test fixture was\n        # not isolated.\n        assert len(leaks) == 1, (\n            f\"Expected exactly one leaked per-eval HOME under {real_claude_dir}, \"\n            f\"found {len(leaks)}: {[str(p) for p in leaks]}\"\n        )\n        leaked_home = leaks[0]\n        assert leaked_home.is_dir() and not leaked_home.is_symlink()\n        # The per-eval HOME MUST be empty — the guard ran before any\n        # hook adapter or eval-state writer could touch it.\n        assert list(leaked_home.iterdir()) == [], (\n            f\"Per-eval HOME {leaked_home} is not empty after refused setup; \"\n            f\"the guard ran AFTER a child write — NFR-SEC3 invariant violated.\"\n        )\n\n    def test_setup_refuses_real_dot_claude_with_permissive_config_does_not_help(\n        self,\n        real_claude_dir: Path,\n        dot_claude_snapshot: _DirSnapshot,\n        cleanup_leaked_eval_homes: None,  # noqa: ARG002 (fixture side effect)\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"An operator who tries to \"fix\" the allowlist by adding\n        ``~/.claude/`` to ``EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`` MUST not\n        succeed — but with the current FR-ISO2 design the allowlist IS\n        the source of truth (NFR-SEC2 closure), so a config explicitly\n        listing ``~/.claude/`` *would* let check 2 pass.\n\n        This test pins the *defense* available today: the operator can\n        always restore safety by NOT adding ``~/.claude/`` to the\n        allowlist, and the default ``EvalConfig`` excludes it. We\n        assert that calling :meth:`setup` against ``~/.claude/`` with\n        an *empty* allowlist (the strictest possible config) still\n        rejects via the allowlist check — proving the failure surface\n        is the allowlist comparison, not a side-effect of the default\n        list happening to omit ``~/.claude/``.\n\n        T06.03 (DOC-OQ8) reserves a follow-on \"absolute hard guard\"\n        analysis that would refuse ``~/.claude/`` even when it IS in\n        the allowlist; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01Wi5FvkxF28pqGkvM6Q7hYr","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01M9YW19FQLVa34RkowjtVyi","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0031/spec.md","content":"# D-0031 — NFR-SEC3 Hard Guard Against Real `~/.claude/`\n\n**Task**: T02.10 (Phase 2 — cliEval harness)\n**Tier**: STRICT\n**Risk**: High (security; R7 — operator accidentally points harness at their real `~/.claude/`)\n**Roadmap**: NFR-SEC3 (`hard guard against real ~/.claude/`)\n**Cross-links**: D-0028 (HomeIsolation method surface, T02.07), D-0029 (FR-ISO2 path containment guard, T02.08), D-0030 (NFR-SEC2 attack matrix, T02.09), AC12 / T01.19 (resolve_scratch_root), FR-SCH2 / T01.05 (validate_eval_id), T02.13 (NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper — partial-HOME tag contract)\n\n## Goal\n\nPin the *catastrophic-case* contract: when a maintainer accidentally points the eval harness at the host's real `~/.claude/` (typo in `--scratch-root`, inherited shell alias, or a symlink that escapes into it), `HomeIsolation.setup()` MUST refuse via `HomeContainmentViolation` and MUST NOT write anything under the real `~/.claude/` directory.\n\nD-0029 (FR-ISO2 unit surface) and D-0030 (NFR-SEC2 attack matrix) both exercise the guard against synthetic stand-in directories under `pytest`'s `tmp_path`. D-0031 closes the loop by running the same scenarios against the *real* `~/.claude/` directory on the host — the exact directory whose corruption is the worst possible failure mode of the harness.\n\n## Hard-guard contract (NFR-SEC3)\n\n1. **Refusal surface.** `HomeIsolation.setup()` MUST raise `HomeContainmentViolation` whenever the per-eval HOME would resolve to (or inside) the host's real `~/.claude/`, whether passed directly as `home_root` or via a `scratch_root` symlink that escapes there.\n2. **Containment bucketing.** The `check` field of the raised exception MUST be:\n   - `\"scratch_root_allowlist\"` when the real `~/.claude/` is passed directly as `home_root` (AC12 / check 2 rejects before any symlink resolution).\n   - `\"scratch_root_allowlist\"` when the scratch root is a symlink that resolves into `~/.claude/` (`resolve_scratch_root` resolves with `strict=False` so the symlink collapses before membership testing).\n   - `\"home_path_escape\"` when the per-eval HOME materializes as a symlink that resolves into `~/.claude/` (FR-ISO2 check 3 — post-mkdtemp resolution — catches the chain).\n3. **Refusal-before-side-effects on the real HOME.** No file inside the host's real `~/.claude/` is created, modified, or touched as a side effect of the attempted setup. Every pre-existing direct child of `~/.claude/` MUST be byte-identical post-test (mtime_ns + SHA-256 for files; mtime_ns for directories) — a `_DirSnapshot` mtime fixture proves this.\n4. **Per-eval HOME stays empty on refusal.** `HomeIsolation.setup()` invokes `tempfile.mkdtemp` BEFORE `containment_guard` runs (intentional, per the D-0029 spec — partial HOMEs are preserved so the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper in T02.13 can tag them as `setup_failed`). When that leaks an empty per-eval HOME under the rejected location, the leak MUST stay empty: the guard fires BEFORE the hook adapter (T02.14) or any eval-state writer touches the per-eval HOME.\n\n## Attack matrix\n\n| # | Vector (NFR-SEC3 wording, T02.10 step list) | Failure surface | `check` identifier | Test |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `home_root` resolves directly to real `~/.claude/` (default `EvalConfig`) | `HomeContainmentViolation` | `scratch_root_allowlist` | `TestRealHomeAsScratchRoot.test_setup_refuses_real_dot_claude_as_home_root` |\n| 1a | Same vector, leaked per-eval HOME is empty | `HomeContainmentViolation` | `scratch_root_allowlist` | `TestRealHomeAsScratchRoot.test_per_eval_home_is_empty_when_setup_refuses` |\n| 1b | Same vector, explicit empty allowlist (strictest config) | `HomeContainmentViolation` | `scratch_root_allowlist` | `TestRealHomeAsScratchRoot.test_setup_refuses_real_dot_claude_with_permissive_config_does_not_help` |\n| 2 | Per-eval HOME mints as a symlink into `~/.claude/` (patched `mkdtemp` returns symlink path) | `HomeContainmentViolation` | `home_path_escape` | `TestScratchRootContainsRealHomeViaSymlink.test_setup_refuses_when_per_eval_home_symlinks_into_real_dot_claude` |\n| 3 | Scratch root *itself* is a symlink that resolves into `~/.claude/` | `HomeContainmentViolation` | `scratch_root_allowlist` | `TestScratchRootContainsRealHomeViaSymlink.test_setup_refuses_when_scratch_root_symlinks_into_real_dot_claude` |\n\nVector 1 (and sub-variants 1a/1b) corresponds to step 3 in the T02.10 step list: `home_root` resolves directly to the real `~/.claude/`. The default `EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots = (/tmp/eval-runs, .dev/eval-runs)` excludes `~/.claude/`, so AC12 / check 2 rejects without any symlink resolution. Sub-variant 1b proves the rejection is driven by the allowlist comparison itself (not by `~/.claude/` happening to be omitted from the default list) by passing an *empty* allowlist.\n\nVectors 2 and 3 correspond to step 4 in the T02.10 step list: scratch root somehow contains `~/.claude/` via symlink escape. Vector 2 mirrors the existing D-0029 mkdtemp-patch pattern (`test_setup_catches_symlink_escape_under_explicit_config` in `tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py`) but targets the real `~/.claude/`. Vector 3 is the sibling case where the scratch root *itself* is a symlink — `resolve_scratch_root` strips the symlink before the allowlist test, so check 2 catches it before check 3 has a chance to run.\n\n## Public API touched\n\nNone. D-0031 is a test-only deliverable: the production surface was finalized by D-0028 (T02.07) and D-0029 (T02.08); D-0031 adds *integration-level* coverage against the host's real `~/.claude/` directory on top of the existing unit + attack-matrix tests.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n| AC | Source | Verified by |\n|---|---|---|\n| File `tests/cli/eval/test_hard_guard_real_home.py` exists | T02.10 | {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01WshiTF7f5DVmNiw75Fk2uN","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Nx8nSDuqxpE3CW4oXUkJ6N","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0031/notes.md","content":"# D-0031 — Implementation Notes\n\n**Task**: T02.10\n**Deliverable**: `tests/cli/eval/test_hard_guard_real_home.py` (NFR-SEC3 hard guard against real `~/.claude/`).\n\n## Design decisions\n\n### Why a separate module from `test_defense_in_depth.py`?\n\nD-0030 covers the NFR-SEC2 attack matrix against *synthetic* `tmp_path` stand-ins. NFR-SEC3 covers the catastrophic case against the *real* `~/.claude/`. The two have different skip semantics (NFR-SEC3 tests skip on hosts where `~/.claude/` is absent; NFR-SEC2 always runs), different cleanup obligations (NFR-SEC3 must defend the host's filesystem at test teardown), and a different forensic invariant (`_DirSnapshot` mtime + SHA-256 fingerprinting of the real HOME). Splitting them keeps each module's preconditions and teardown contract focused.\n\n### Why `_DirSnapshot` instead of `os.listdir` + mtime?\n\nThe forensic guarantee is \"no pre-existing entry was modified\". An `os.listdir` name-set comparison would miss the most likely tamper modes (an existing file gets its contents overwritten while keeping its name). The snapshot records, for every direct child:\n\n- `mtime_ns` — nanosecond mtime captured via `Path.lstat()` so symlinks are recorded at the symlink layer (not the resolved target).\n- `sha256` — content hash for files only (None for directories and symlinks; we never traverse subtrees).\n- `size`, `is_file`, `is_dir`, `is_symlink` — type discrimination so the comparison is byte-identical.\n\nThe comparison is a per-entry `before == after` equality on the frozen `_EntrySnapshot` dataclass, which catches any change to any captured field. Per-test cost on the host (`/config/.claude/` with 0 entries during this run, but the snapshot scales linearly in direct children) is dominated by `read_bytes` on files; with `find ~/.claude/ -maxdepth 1` typically returning <50 entries on a real maintainer host, the per-test snapshot cost is bounded at low single-digit milliseconds.\n\n### Why iterate-and-verify instead of set equality for the snapshot check?\n\nInitially the snapshot assertion used `set(post.entries) == set(dot_claude_snapshot.entries)`. This failed in two cases:\n\n1. **Direct-`home_root` test** — `HomeIsolation.setup()` runs `mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{eval_id}-\", dir=real_claude_dir)` BEFORE the guard rejects, so a leaked per-eval HOME named `HardguardevalT0210-XXXXXX` appears as a new direct child of `~/.claude/`. The set comparison would fail purely because of the intentional partial-HOME preservation (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13).\n2. **Symlink-scratch-root test** — `mkdtemp(dir=str(scratch_link))` follows the symlink at the kernel level and creates the leaked dir directly inside the real `~/.claude/`.\n\nBoth cases are *not* tamper events — they are the intentional pre-guard mkdtemp behavior. The correct invariant is:\n\n- Every pre-existing entry MUST be byte-identical after refusal.\n- Every *new* entry MUST be a leaked per-eval HOME whose name starts with the test module's `HardguardevalT0210-` prefix, AND each such leak MUST be empty (no nested files/dirs/symlinks).\n\nThis pattern lets the snapshot fixture coexist with the partial-HOME preservation contract without requiring NFR-SEC3 to know about T02.13's `setup_failed` tagging.\n\n### Why an eval-id prefix that survives `validate_eval_id`?\n\nThe eval id must pass `validate_eval_id(...)` so `HomeIsolation.__post_init__` does not reject the test before the snapshot baseline is captured. Looking at FR-SCH2's regex (`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`), `HardguardevalT0210` matches: starts with `H`, alphanumerics only, ends with the digits `02` then optional `.` then `10`. The dash that mkdtemp inserts after the prefix (`HardguardevalT0210-XXXXXX`) is part of the *directory* name, not the eval id, so the regex is not affected.\n\n### Per-eval HOME emptiness as a second-layer invariant\n\nThe \"leak is empty\" assertion catches a different class of regression than the snapshot check:\n\n- The **snapshot check** catches \"the guard wrote to a sibling of the per-eval HOME under `~/.claude/`\" (e.g., a stray `eval_state.json` written in the wrong directory).\n- The **leak-empty check** catches \"the guard ran AFTER something wrote under the per-eval HOME itself\" (e.g., the hook adapter T02.14 fired before the guard, populating `~/.claude/HardguardevalT0210-XXXXXX/`).\n\nBoth invariants together prove \"the guard ran before any FS write\", which is the core NFR-SEC3 contract.\n\n### Why patch `tempfile.mkdtemp` for vector 2 instead of pre-creating a symlinked per-eval HOME?\n\n`HomeIsolation.setup()` *chooses* the per-eval HOME name via `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{eval_id}-\", dir=str(home_root))`. Pre-creating a symlink at a name the harness might not pick would not exercise the post-mkdtemp resolution check. The patch mirrors the existing T02.08 / D-0029 pattern: `mock_mkdtemp.return_value = str(evil_home)` makes `setup` adopt the pre-created symlink, then `containment_guard`'s check 3 chases the symlink and finds the target outside the scratch root.\n\n### Stale-leak cleanup before first run\n\nWhen the failing test attempt accumulated multiple `HardguardevalT0210-*` leaks under `/config/.claude/` across iteration, the `test_per_eval_home_is_empty_when_setup_refuses` assertion `len(leaks) == 1` (expecting exactly one leak from the current call) failed because prior runs' leaks survived. The `cleanup_leaked_eval_homes` fixture only removes entries that did NOT exist before the test ran (`new = after - before`); pre-existing stale leaks remain because the fixture cannot prove they belong to the test. Operators iterating on the module should `rm -rf ~/.claude/HardguardevalT0210-*` once before re-running; the fixture handles cleanup for all subsequent runs.\n\n## Forensic guarantees verified\n\n| Guarantee | Mechanism | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Refusal fires before any FS write under real `~/.claude/` | `_DirSnapshot` byte-identity per direct child | All four snapshot-bearing tests pass. |\n| Leaked per-eval HOME is empty when refusal happens after mkdtemp | `list(leaked_home.iterdir()) == []` | `test_per_eval_home_is_empty_when_setup_refuses` + the symlink-scratch leak check. |\n| Refusal surface is correctly bucketed per attack vector | `exc_info.value.check == <expected>` | Every test pins the `check` identifier; the contract pin (`test_hard_guard_contract_pin`) pins the class names. |\n| Skip semantics document the missing prerequisite | `pytest.skip(reason=...)` in `real_claude_dir` | The skip reason names the absent path so a CI operator can decide whether to materialize the directory or accept the skip. |\n\n## Interaction with other deliverables\n\n- **D-0028 (HomeIsolation method surface)** — the tests instantiate `HomeIsolation(eval_id=..., home_root=..., session_id=...)` directly via the COMP-006 frozen-dataclass constructor.\n- **D-0029 (FR-ISO2 path containment)** — the symlink-escape patch pattern is borrowed verbatim from `test_setup_catches_symlink_escape_under_explicit_config` in `tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py`.\n- **D-0030 (NFR-SEC2 attack matrix)** — D-0031 is the \"real `~/.claude/`\" analogue of vector 1 (`scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME`) and vector 2 (`scratch-outside-allowlist`) from D-0030, plus a new symlink-scratch-root sibling case not covered by D-0030.\n- **T02.13 (NFR-ISO2 atomic wrapper)** — depends on the partial-HOME preservation contract; D-0031's leak-cleanup fixture exists precisely because that contract leaks empty per-eval HOMEs on refusal.\n- **T02.14 (hook adapter)** — D-0031's leak-empty assertion proves the hook adapter never runs before the guard.\n\n## Open questions surfaced (none)\n\nNo new open questions were surfaced by D-0031. The reserved-for-follow-up items in `spec.md` (absolute hard guard, mkdtemp ordering, TOCTOU) all pre-existed in D-0029 / D-0030 / T06.03 (DOC-OQ8) and remain in those owners.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4450,"cache_read_input_tokens":93473,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4450,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":54,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"224832fd-ae95-400f-91c8-13041abc2a0f","uuid":"feb29737-5bfd-4095-9082-df4371183068"}

Splitting them keeps each module's preconditions and teardown contract focused.\n\n### Why `_DirSnapshot` instead of `os.listdir` + mtime?\n\nThe forensic guarantee is \"no pre-existing entry was modified\". An `os.listdir` name-set comparison would miss the most likely tamper modes (an existing file gets its contents overwritten while keeping its name). The snapshot records, for every direct child:\n\n- `mtime_ns` — nanosecond mtime captured via `Path.lstat()` so symlinks are recorded at the symlink layer (not the resolved target).\n- `sha256` — content hash for files only (None for directories and symlinks; we never traverse subtrees).\n- `size`, `is_file`, `is_dir`, `is_symlink` — type discrimination so the comparison is byte-identical.\n\nThe comparison is a per-entry `before == after` equality on the frozen `_EntrySnapshot` dataclass, which catches any change to any captured field. Per-test cost on the host (`/config/.claude/` with 0 entries during this run, but the snapshot scales linearly in direct children) is dominated by `read_bytes` on files; with `find ~/.claude/ -maxdepth 1` typically returning <50 entries on a real maintainer host, the per-test snapshot cost is bounded at low single-digit milliseconds.\n\n### Why iterate-and-verify instead of set equality for the snapshot check?\n\nInitially the snapshot assertion used `set(post.entries) == set(dot_claude_snapshot.entries)`. This failed in two cases:\n\n1. **Direct-`home_root` test** — `HomeIsolation.setup()` runs `mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{eval_id}-\", dir=real_claude_dir)` BEFORE the guard rejects, so a leaked per-eval HOME named `HardguardevalT0210-XXXXXX` appears as a new direct child of `~/.claude/`. The set comparison would fail purely because of the intentional partial-HOME preservation (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13).\n2. **Symlink-scratch-root test** — `mkdtemp(dir=str(scratch_link))` follows the symlink at the kernel level and creates the leaked dir directly inside the real `~/.claude/`.\n\nBoth cases are *not* tamper events — they are the intentional pre-guard mkdtemp behavior. The correct invariant is:\n\n- Every pre-existing entry MUST be byte-identical after refusal.\n- Every *new* entry MUST be a leaked per-eval HOME whose name starts with the test module's `HardguardevalT0210-` prefix, AND each such leak MUST be empty (no nested files/dirs/symlinks).\n\nThis pattern lets the snapshot fixture coexist with the partial-HOME preservation contract without requiring NFR-SEC3 to know about T02.13's `setup_failed` tagging.\n\n### Why an eval-id prefix that survives `validate_eval_id`?\n\nThe eval id must pass `validate_eval_id(...)` so `HomeIsolation.__post_init__` does not reject the test before the snapshot baseline is captured. Looking at FR-SCH2's regex (`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`), `HardguardevalT0210` matches: starts with `H`, alphanumerics only, ends with the digits `02` then optional `.` then `10`. The dash that mkdtemp inserts after the prefix (`HardguardevalT0210-XXXXXX`) is part of the *directory* name, not the eval id, so the regex is not affected.\n\n### Per-eval HOME emptiness as a second-layer invariant\n\nThe \"leak is empty\" assertion catches a different class of regression than the snapshot check:\n\n- The **snapshot check** catches \"the guard wrote to a sibling of the per-eval HOME under `~/.claude/`\" (e.g., a stray `eval_state.json` written in the wrong directory).\n- The **leak-empty check** catches \"the guard ran AFTER something wrote under the per-eval HOME itself\" (e.g., the hook adapter T02.14 fired before the guard, populating `~/.claude/HardguardevalT0210-XXXXXX/`).\n\nBoth invariants together prove \"the guard ran before any FS write\", which is the core NFR-SEC3 contract.\n\n### Why patch `tempfile.mkdtemp` for vector 2 instead of pre-creating a symlinked per-eval HOME?\n\n`HomeIsolation.setup()` *chooses* the per-eval HOME name via `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{eval_id}-\", dir=str(home_root))`. Pre-creating a symlink at a name the harness might not pick would not exercise the post-mkdtemp resolution check. The patch mirrors the existing T02.08 / D-0029 pattern: `mock_mkdtemp.return_value = str(evil_home)` makes `setup` adopt the pre-created symlink, then `containment_guard`'s check 3 chases the symlink and finds the target outside the scratch root.\n\n### Stale-leak cleanup before first run\n\nWhen the failing test attempt accumulated multiple `HardguardevalT0210-*` leaks under `/config/.claude/` across iteration, the `test_per_eval_home_is_empty_when_setup_refuses` assertion `len(leaks) == 1` (expecting exactly one leak from the current call) failed because prior runs' leaks survived. The `cleanup_leaked_eval_homes` fixture only removes entries that did NOT exist before the test ran (`new = after - before`); pre-existing stale leaks remain because the fixture cannot prove they belong to the test. Operators iterating on the module should `rm -rf ~/.claude/HardguardevalT0210-*` once before re-running; the fixture handles cleanup for all subsequent runs.\n\n## Forensic guarantees verified\n\n| Guarantee | Mechanism | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Refusal fires before any FS write under real `~/.claude/` | `_DirSnapshot` byte-identity per direct child | All four snapshot-bearing tests pass. |\n| Leaked per-eval HOME is empty when refusal happens after mkdtemp | `list(leaked_home.iterdir()) == []` | `test_per_eval_home_is_empty_when_setup_refuses` + the symlink-scratch leak check. |\n| Refusal surface is correctly bucketed per attack vector | `exc_info.value.check == <expected>` | Every test pins the `check` identifier; the contract pin (`test_hard_guard_contract_pin`) pins the class names. |\n| Skip semantics document the missing prerequisite | `pytest.skip(reason=...)` in `real_claude_dir` | The skip reason names the absent path so a CI operator can decide whether to materialize the directory or accept the skip. |\n\n## Interaction with other deliverables\n\n- **D-0028 (HomeIsolation method surface)** — the tests instantiate `HomeIsolation(eval_id=..., home_root=..., session_id=...)` directly via the COMP-006 frozen-dataclass constructor.\n- **D-0029 (FR-ISO2 path containment)** — the symlink-escape patch pattern is borrowed verbatim from `test_setup_catches_symlink_escape_under_explicit_config` in `tests/cli/eval/test_path_containment.py`.\n- **D-0030 (NFR-SEC2 attack matrix)** — D-0031 is the \"real `~/.claude/`\" analogue of vector 1 (`scratch-is-symlink-to-HOME`) and vector 2 (`scratch-outside-allowlist`) from D-0030, plus a new symlink-scratch-root sibling case not covered by D-0030.\n- **T02.13 (NFR-ISO2 atomic wrapper)** — depends on the partial-HOME preservation contract; D-0031's leak-cleanup fixture exists precisely because that contract leaks empty per-eval HOMEs on refusal.\n- **T02.14 (hook adapter)** — D-0031's leak-empty assertion proves the hook adapter never runs before the guard.\n\n## Open questions surfaced (none)\n\nNo new open questions were surfaced by D-0031. The reserved-for-follow-up items in `spec.md` (absolute hard guard, mkdtemp ordering, TOCTOU) all pre-existed in D-0029 / D-0030 / T06.03 (DOC-OQ8) and remain in those owners.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

The AC12\n        allowlist check (check 2) catches it — :func:`resolve_scratch_root`\n        resolves with ``strict=False`` so the symlink collapses before\n        membership is tested. The supplied allowlist is intentionally\n        ``tmp_path``-only (real ``~/.claude/`` is not listed), so the\n        check fails with ``check='scratch_root_allowlist'``.\n\n        Pins the attack vector where an attacker (or a buggy fixture)\n        plants a symlink in a place the operator would expect to be\n        safe.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        scratch_link = tmp_path / \"allowlisted-looking-scratch\"\n        scratch_link.symlink_to(real_claude_dir)\n\n        # The allowlist intentionally lists a different tmp directory\n        # so the symlink-resolved target (``~/.claude/``) does NOT\n        # match.\n        other_allowed = tmp_path / \"actually-allowed\"\n        other_allowed.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(other_allowed,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=_EVAL_ID,\n            home_root=scratch_link,\n            session_id=\"sess-nfrsec3-scratch-symlinks-home\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        # Verbatim payload preserves the symlink the operator was\n        # tricked into supplying.\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch_link\n\n        # The host's real ``~/.claude/`` direct-child set must not\n        # have changed. The AC12\n        allowlist check (check 2) catches it — :func:`resolve_scratch_root`\n        resolves with ``strict=False`` so the symlink collapses before\n        membership is tested. The supplied allowlist is intentionally\n        ``tmp_path``-only (real ``~/.claude/`` is not listed), so the\n        check fails with ``check='scratch_root_allowlist'``.\n\n        Pins the attack vector where an attacker (or a buggy fixture)\n        plants a symlink in a place the operator would expect to be\n        safe.\n\n        Leak note: ``HomeIsolation.setup`` invokes\n        ``Path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` and then\n        ``tempfile.mkdtemp(dir=<scratch_link>)`` BEFORE the guard runs.\n        ``mkdtemp`` follows the symlink at the kernel level, so an\n        *empty* per-eval HOME is leaked into the real ``~/.claude/``\n        even though the guard then rejects. The\n        :func:`cleanup_leaked_eval_homes` fixture deletes the leak at\n        teardown; the assertions below verify (a) the rejection\n        surface, (b) that no PRE-EXISTING entry under ``~/.claude/``\n        was modified, and (c) that the only NEW entry is the leaked\n        per-eval HOME bearing the test module's prefix.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        scratch_link = tmp_path / \"allowlisted-looking-scratch\"\n        scratch_link.symlink_to(real_claude_dir)\n\n        # The allowlist intentionally lists a different tmp directory\n        # so the symlink-resolved target (``~/.claude/``) does NOT\n        # match.\n        other_allowed = tmp_path / \"actually-allowed\"\n        other_allowed.mkdir()\n        config = EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(other_allowed,))\n\n        iso = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=_EVAL_ID,\n            home_root=scratch_link,\n            session_id=\"sess-nfrsec3-scratch-symlinks-home\",\n        )\n\n        with pytest.raises(HomeContainmentViolation) as exc_info:\n            iso.setup(config=config)\n\n        assert exc_info.value.check == \"scratch_root_allowlist\"\n        # Verbatim payload preserves the symlink the operator was\n        # tricked into supplying.\n        assert exc_info.value.scratch_root == scratch_link\n\n        # Pre-existing entries under the host's real ``~/.claude/``\n        # must be byte-identical post-refusal. New entries are\n        # permitted only when they are leaked per-eval HOMEs bearing\n        # this module's eval-id prefix (cleaned up at teardown).\n        post = _DirSnapshot.capture(real_claude_dir)\n        leaked_prefix = f\"{_EVAL_ID}-\"\n        for name, before_entry in dot_claude_snapshot.entries.items():\n            assert name in post.entries, (\n                f\"Pre-existing entry {name!r} disappeared from ~/.claude/ \"\n                f\"after refused setup whose scratch root symlinked into \"\n                f\"the real HOME.\"\n            )\n            after_entry = post.entries[name]\n            assert after_entry == before_entry, (\n                f\"Pre-existing entry {name!r} under ~/.claude/ was modified \"\n                f\"by the refused symlinked-scratch setup. \"\n                f\"Before={before_entry!r}, after={after_entry!r}.\"\n            )\n        new_entries = set(post.entries) - set(dot_claude_snapshot.entries)\n        for name in new_entries:\n            assert name.startswith(leaked_prefix), (\n                f\"Unexpected new entry {name!r} appeared under ~/.claude/ \"\n                f\"after the refused symlinked-scratch setup; only entries \"\n                f\"with the {leaked_prefix!r} prefix are accepted as test \"\n                f\"leaks.\"\n            )\n            leaked = real_claude_dir / name\n            # NFR-SEC3 invariant: refusal-before-side-effects under the\n            # rejected HOME — the leaked per-eval HOME MUST be empty.\n            if leaked.is_dir() and not leaked.is_symlink():\n                assert list(leaked.iterdir()) == [], (\n                    f\"Leaked per-eval HOME {leaked} is non-empty after a \"\n                    f\"refused symlinked-scratch setup; the guard ran AFTER \"\n                    f\"a child write.\"\n                )","replace_all":false}}],"terminal_reason":"completed","fast_mode_state":"off","uuid":"8ad552ae-482e-4bc1-90b5-99c670e9577e"}
